Pictures are cached least-recently-used and bounded by the same memory-derived picture budget every other picture the console holds is bounded by (§Pane kinds, the `inspector` bullet), keyed on the sanitized source plus a palette identity that changes exactly when the palette or the label face does, with the identifier baked into a cached picture rebased on every hit so two copies of one diagram never share one internal reference; the source is sanitized first (byte-order mark, zero-width and control characters, line endings, entities, smart quotes). The picture is drawn again on a theme change and on a text-size change, and its floor height is derived from the line it is read at. A diagram opens into the quick look and nowhere else — there is no second viewer — where a pinch or a modifier-wheel zooms about the pointer and a drag pans; the block's own hover actions copy it as a picture or copy its source. |
| Terminal | ADOPT-with-constraints `@xterm/xterm` 6.0.0 with the WebGL, fit, search, unicode11, serialize, and web-links addons; OWN-BUILD the React wrapper, the renderer pool, and the link scheme guard | Pool WebGL panes and never churn the addon (a disposed WebGL addon does not release its context, and Chromium drops the oldest context past sixteen); `onContextLoss` falls back to DOM; `allowProposedApi` only for Unicode 11; the terminal's own flow control is propagated to the process behind it, which is the library's own answer to a shell that outruns the renderer, so the write buffer's watermark is the safety net rather than the mechanism; the printed-URL link provider is the project's own `@xterm/addon-web-links` 0.12.0 rather than a hand-written one — it carries the buffer-index back-mapping (wrapped lines, early-wrapped wide characters, the expansion bound) and its default matcher admits `http`/`https` only, the same two schemes the own-built guard allows — while both link paths, OSC 8 and printed, run that guard on activation beside `allowNonHttpProtocols: false`; `disableStdin` plus wire-level gating for watchers. AVOID every published React wrapper and the canvas addon. |
| ANSI in transcript rows | ADOPT `anser` (`ansiToJson` only); own span mapper | JSON chunks mapped to Meridian tokens; never an HTML-string path. AVOID ansi-to-react, ansi-to-html. |
| Voice call playback | ADOPT-with-constraints `signalsmith-stretch` 1.3.2 (MIT) in the window, in its own AudioWorklet node | A Codex hold's request plays into the call at 1.5× with its pitch kept, each pause over 0.2 s cut to 0.2 s, and never through a media element, whose sound reaches Web Audio 1.1 s late in WebKit and loses its first 80 ms, where the stretch starts in about 30 ms on every engine. The request streams in from the release a second at a time: raised from 24 kHz to the call's 48 kHz audio context by writing each sample and then the midpoint to the next, passed in with `addBuffers` one second ahead of the read position, its memory moved rather than copied, let go with `dropBuffers` once wholly half a second behind, and topped up on the stretch's own position reports three times a second, never on a page timer a hidden window slows, so the stretch holds under 0.6 MB and the whole hold at most 6.4 MB, the kept request at most 5.8 MB of it. A held request playing in adds at most 1.5% of one core, a figure taken with two other cores busy, since on an otherwise idle machine the processor's slower clock makes the same light audio work read about three times higher. The package carries a `pnpm patch` to its read loop, which in 1.3.2 loses its place at the first joint between pieces (upstream issue #22; the fix is posted as Signalsmith-Audio/signalsmith-stretch#30 and the patch stays until a release carries it), and the repository's MIT notice is carried beside it, since the package ships none. AVOID a buffer source's playback rate, which shifts the pitch. |
| Electron runtime | ADOPT Electron 44.x | Electron 41 is end-of-life; the `WebContentsView` detached-bounds fix landed on 42 and later only (§Electron Version And Support Window). |
| Native browser view | ADOPT `WebContentsView`; OWN-BUILD the bounds bridge, the airspace policy (hide the view and swap in a `capturePage` image while an overlay is open), focus hand-off, and a `devtools-protocol`-typed `webContents.debugger` wrapper | Bounds scale by `webContents.getZoomFactor()`; the pane is a native view, never an iframe (`frame-src 'none'` stands). The window that hosts it is a `BaseWindow` and never a `BrowserWindow` (§Security Hardening Baseline), and the subsystem is [ADR-034](../decisions/034-embedded-browser-for-preview.md)'s. A captured page's scale is read from the returned image rather than from the platform's reported scale factors, which report a single factor against a genuinely doubled capture. |
| Browser tool set | ADOPT-with-constraints `playwright-core`'s own tool server — `tools.createConnection` from its `lib/coreBundle` export on the stable pin, the function the `@playwright/mcp` shim re-exports — hosted **inside the daemon** with no `@playwright/mcp` package and no alpha Playwright in the tree, one connection per session handed that session's browser context, on the daemon's own loopback HTTP route in the SDK transport's stateful session mode (the stateless mode refuses the client's initialized notification), refusing any request that carries an `Origin` header or names another path ([ADR-034](../decisions/034-embedded-browser-for-preview.md)); ADOPT `playwright-core` 1.63.0 (Apache-2.0) for the daemon's headless fallback and its element-reference selector engine, and `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.30.0 (MIT) as a direct daemon dependency for hosting that tool server in process; ADOPT `perfect-freehand` 1.2.3 (MIT, 112 kB unpacked, no runtime dependencies) in the renderer for the mark strokes | The server never owns or launches a browser. On the desktop it reaches the Preview pages through the daemon's relay over Electron's in-process debugger, and there is no debug port on either host: main attaches `webContents.debugger` to each Preview view and to no other `webContents`, reports each view as `preview.pageTargetReport {pageId, targetId}`, carries the daemon's commands from `preview.pageDebuggerSend {pageId, message}` and returns replies and events as `preview.pageDebuggerReport {pageId, message}`, and Playwright joins with `connectOverCDP(transport)`. The relay joins the pages into one browser whose target list is exactly the open Preview pages, every console window absent, and its page-session filter is a hard requirement: on a page's session only `Target.setAutoAttach`, detaching its own children and `Target.getTargetInfo` for its own target pass, and every other `Target.*` and `Browser.*` command is refused, because a page's debugger reaches every target in the app. Off the desktop the connection is handed the headless browser's context. No `--remote-debugging-port` and no `--remote-debugging-pipe` is set on either host. The agent's `resize` gets exactly the size it asked for, fitted into the pane by main, and every Electron bump re-runs the resize probe, because the fit uses experimental protocol fields. Zero stdio children and zero extra Chromium. The connection is the session's one tool entry on the daemon's route — on Claude Code passed through `--mcp-config` at start with the process started under `--strict-mcp-config`, on Codex `mcp_servers.<id>.url` in the conversation's configuration at `thread/start`, with `default_tools_approval_mode = "approve"` and a `tool_timeout_sec` above the longest bounded wait, passed again at every fork — so the route must be listening before the provider process starts; `Browser tools for sidekicks` off leaves the browser tools out of that entry while it stays for the daemon's other tools, and `browser_run_code_unsafe` is left out of the tool list, because it runs code in the daemon's browser tool process outside the provider's sandbox and the command approvals. The two providers' call shapes normalize to one transcript row. Pin `playwright-core` exactly and never at latest: the `lib/coreBundle` export and the element-reference format are re-checked on every bump, and a bump that fails the check does not land; should the export ever leave the package, the fallback is the `@playwright/mcp` shim held to the stable pair by a pnpm override, never its own alpha pin. |
| Packaging and update | ADOPT `electron-builder` 26.15.x and `electron-updater` 6.8.9 as shipped, with its stock GitHub provider, its SHA-512 check of the download and the operating system's code signature on macOS and Windows (§Build And Packaging, §Auto-Updater) | AVOID update-electron-app, the built-in `autoUpdater` alone, Electron Forge, and `electron-updater`'s own Debian and RPM updaters (they bypass the repository's signature). |
| Main-process storage and logging | OWN-BUILD main's own files (the appearance record, the keyboard map, the window places and the updater state: atomic rename, Zod parse, fail-closed defaults) and a JSONL logger; ADOPT `@electron/fuses` | Main holds no credential material and seals nothing, so it loads no keystore library and no `safeStorage`; every secret is the background service's (§Native Keystore). AVOID electron-store, electron-log. |
| Tests | ADOPT-with-constraints Vitest browser mode via `@vitest/browser-playwright`; ADOPT `@playwright/test`, `@testing-library/react`, knip, memlab; ADOPT `size-limit` 13.x for the bundle-size budget test (the brotli and gzip sizes of the built `out/renderer` assets, budgets in its one config, on the tier-1 desktop job) — a maintained library measures the same budget, so the project keeps no own-built test for it; ADOPT bare `axe-core` as a test-only devDependency — the accessibility tier runs in-page, and browser-mode test code receives Vitest's own `page` locator object rather than Playwright's `Page` ("your tests don't have access to Playwright's `page` because it runs on the server"), while the Playwright `Page` that `@axe-core/playwright`'s `AxeBuilder({ page })` requires is reachable only inside a server-side custom command, where it is the orchestrator HTML wrapping the test iframe rather than the component under test | `axe-core` is MPL-2.0 — outside the project's MIT / Apache / BSD / ISC dependency norm — and is admitted for a never-distributed test dependency only, recorded in [ADR-019 §License](../decisions/019-v1-deployment-model-and-oss-license.md#license). AVOID jsdom, lost-pixel (archived), hosted visual-regression services, msw. **No pixel gate is registered**: §Console Test Tiers carries no pixel comparison, so the visual-regression evaluation in §References records why no hosted service would be adopted rather than what a build is compared against. |
| Diff viewer | ADOPT `diff` 9.0.0 (jsdiff, BSD-3-Clause) for parse and intraline compute; OWN-BUILD one shared virtualized diff-row renderer for inline cards and the pane, painting the color spans the daemon computed | No candidate is both headless and virtualized. jsdiff parses an 898 kB patch in 2.1 ms; line diffs above about 2,000 lines run off-thread. No coloring happens in the renderer at all, in a worker or otherwise — the daemon is the one colorer ([ADR-033](../decisions/033-one-syntax-colorer-in-the-daemon.md)), and the cost of doing it here is why: 10,000 TypeScript lines tokenized on the main thread cost 1,960 ms and 84.5 MB of heap. AVOID the highlight.js-bundling diff viewers (315 kB gzip), diff2html, react-diff-viewer-continued, `@codemirror/merge`, monaco, diff-match-patch (archived). |
| Node-graph builder | ADOPT-with-constraints `@xyflow/react` 12.12.0 ([ADR-024 §Decision](../decisions/024-visual-node-graph-workflow-authoring.md#decision) holds); ADOPT `@dagrejs/dagre` 3.1.1 (MIT, 1.4 MB unpacked) for the layout, left to right, in the renderer and in the daemon alike; ADOPT `croner` 10.0.1 (MIT, 155 kB unpacked, no dependencies, Node ≥ 18) in the daemon for workflow schedules and Node's own `fs.watch` with `picomatch` 4.0.7 (MIT, 94 kB unpacked) for the file-watch trigger; OWN-BUILD node and edge visuals, inspector, keyboard connect mode, layout persistence | MIT; 59.4 kB gzip plus the required 13.6 kB `base.css` whose 41 tokens are all set from Meridian; 9.1 MB heap and 60 fps drag at 200 nodes. Exact-pin 12.12.0 with `@xyflow/system` in lockstep (12.11.4 shipped broken); never alias `zustand`; `isValidConnection` is not applied by `addEdges`, so every programmatic and keyboard edge path calls the same predicate. The layout is the same library on both sides of the wire, so a definition an agent wrote with no layout at all still opens laid out and the two processes agree on where a node sits; layout bytes are still not definition bytes ([Spec-015 §Canvas layout is not definition bytes (SA-34)](./015-workflow-authoring-and-execution.md#canvas-layout-is-not-definition-bytes-sa-34)). `croner`, `picomatch` and the watch are new daemon dependencies rather than renderer ones, and the trigger surface they serve is [Spec-015 §Entry node and the V1 trigger surface (SA-36)](./015-workflow-authoring-and-execution.md#entry-node-and-the-v1-trigger-surface-sa-36). AVOID elkjs (EPL / GPL, 440 kB), the unmaintained `dagre` 0.8.5 line the `@dagrejs` fork supersedes, d3-dag, graphology, and a scheduler or a file watcher of our own. |
| Layout, panes, drag | ADOPT-with-constraints `react-resizable-panels` 4.14.1 for the pane tracks and `@atlaskit/pragmatic-drag-and-drop` 4.0.0 for drag; ADOPT `overlayscrollbars` 2.16.0 with `overlayscrollbars-react` 0.5.6 for every scroller but the transcript; OWN-BUILD the pane store, separator chrome, keyboard and menu reorder paths, live-region strings, native-pane rect tracking, and toasts | Panels: MIT, 11.7 kB gzip, no CSS, ARIA window-splitter with keyboard; store-owned layout. The ARIA min/max swap on three-plus pane groups is fixed upstream from 4.12.4; from that release a pane reorder threw ("Panel constraints not found for index 2") because the group keeps panels in registration order while it reads separators in page order, so 4.14.1 ships with a two-line `pnpm patch` that re-sorts both on each group mount, and a real-browser test moves a pane; the patch comes out when a release fixes upstream issue #758, and stepping back to 4.12.3 was declined because it restores the swap. Drag: Apache-2.0, headless, native HTML5 drag so no per-frame renders; it provides no keyboard drag by design, so the menu and chord paths are ours; the rows it drags are the waiting messages stacked under a running turn and the terminal's and Preview pane's tabs, never a pane, because the column holds one main pane and carries no tab strip. Scrollbars: both MIT, the core 3.0 MB unpacked with no dependencies, the React binding asking for React 16.8 or newer and a 2.x core; set to hide once the pointer leaves, themed from the console's own tokens for bar width, fade and hover thickening, and drawn over the content so no scroller takes layout width for one in any state. The transcript draws none, its right edge being the ask rail's. AVOID dockview, flexlayout-react, rc-dock, allotment (mouse-only), react-mosaic, `@dnd-kit/*`, react-dnd, and every toast library that injects its own stylesheet. |
| Headless UI primitives | ADOPT-with-constraints `@base-ui/react` 1.8.0 as the one widget family; ADOPT `tinykeys` 4.0.1 as the chord parser only; ADOPT `@floating-ui/react-dom` 2.1.9 (MIT, 64 kB) for our own non-widget overlays, added only when a surface outside the widget family needs it, and none of the design's overlays does today, the quick look, the reader and the palette being dialogs; OWN-BUILD the keybinding service and its typed condition tree, the command registry and palette, the one fuzzy scorer (own-built, in `packages/search-ranking`), the live announcer, roving tabindex for transcript rows, and the modal `inert` guard | The only family complete for the console's widget list, including combobox and autocomplete, with zero CSS and subpath exports (89.6 kB gzip for the console set). Exact pin; subpath imports only; every popup portals into our overlay root; no body scroll lock; the console applies `inert` to the app root while a modal is open until the upstream focus defect closes. Runner-up `react-aria-components` with a named flip trigger (a vendor screen-reader matrix becoming a sign-off requirement). AVOID Ariakit, Headless UI, cmdk, kbar, react-hotkeys-hook, focus-trap-react, react-focus-lock. |
| State, storage, forms, search, dates | ADOPT-with-constraints zustand 5 (one store per open session, partitioned entity maps, per-frame event coalescing, per-row selectors, no `persist` or `immer` on the event path); ADOPT-with-constraints `idb` 8.0.3 under an own per-session partition, LRU trim, and quota gauge; OWN-BUILD the JSON Schema field mapper and schema form on Zod `fromJSONSchema`; rank with the one fuzzy scorer in `packages/search-ranking` (`@ai-sidekicks/search-ranking`), shared by the palette, Settings search, the keyboard page, the sessions search and the daemon's `@` file search; the transcript's Find and the diff pane's file filter stay substring, as their designs say; ADOPT native `Intl` and `Temporal` | §Security Hardening Baseline's CSP has no `unsafe-eval`, so every schema library that compiles validators at runtime (Ajv, and with it rjsf, JSON Forms, and jsoneditor validation) is disqualified before size is weighed. A flat entity map costs 1.3 ms per event at 20,000 entities and a partitioned one 57 µs. Temporal is renderer-only — the main process and every `node`-environment test project use `Date` and ISO strings, because the toolchain Node the `main-unit` project runs under has no `Temporal` (probe row below); contracts stay on ISO strings and epoch milliseconds. AVOID jotai, valtio, mobx, immer, idb-keyval, dexie, localforage, `@rjsf/*`, `@jsonforms/*`, ajv, CodeMirror, fuse.js, fzf, minisearch, date-fns, dayjs, both Temporal polyfills. |
| Motion, fonts, icons, meters, formatting | ADOPT platform motion (CSS transitions, `@starting-style`, View Transitions, the Web Animations API); OWN-BUILD a spring sampler emitting `linear()` easings; ADOPT-with-constraints `unplugin-icons` with `@iconify-json/tabler` plus our own faces in the same collection, compiled at build time; ADOPT IBM Plex Sans and IBM Plex Mono variable builds self-hosted from the foundry packages; OWN-BUILD the meters, sparklines, and arcs as SVG and CSS; ADOPT native `Intl` for numbers, currency, bytes, durations, and relative time | An animation library on the render path fights the virtualizer (layout measurement per render, own frame loops, per-child observers). Lucide is the default icon set of the most common component kit, so it reads as the generic dashboard look. The faces come from the foundry packages. AVOID `motion` as a layer, `react-spring`, `@formkit/auto-animate`, Inter, Geist, commercial monospace faces, uplot, visx, recharts, nivo, `pretty-ms`, `filesize`, `bytes`. |
| Structure enforcement | ADOPT knip 6.39.0 (the same tool the Tests row already adopts for dead exports; this row states its scope and its gate and does not re-admit it); ADOPT-with-constraints `dependency-cruiser` 18.5.0; the third gate is the desktop package's own `lint` over the ESLint flat config [ADR-021](../decisions/021-v1-toolchain-selection.md) already admits repo-wide, carrying ADOPT-with-constraints `eslint-plugin-perfectionist` 5.12.1 (MIT) | The third leg admits exactly **one** library: the structural rules are `no-restricted-imports` / `no-restricted-syntax` / `no-restricted-globals` / `no-restricted-properties` entries in `apps/desktop/eslint.config.mjs`, which add no dependency, no command, and no CI step of their own, and `eslint-plugin-perfectionist` joins them inside that same gate — no new command, no new CI step, one devDependency. No ESLint plugin was adopted for the one-component-per-`.tsx` rule: the nearest candidate, `eslint-plugin-react-refresh`'s `only-export-components`, checks a different property — that a module exporting a component exports only components, a Fast Refresh constraint rather than a count — so that rule stays a review rule stated in `apps/desktop/AGENTS.md`. ADOPT-with-constraints `eslint-plugin-perfectionist` — exactly **two** rules, both scoped to `src/renderer/src/**` and both run `type: 'unsorted'`, so the claim is the order of the `apps/desktop/AGENTS.md` §Module shape SECTIONS and never an alphabet: `sort-modules` ranks the exported declaration kinds (types and interfaces → class → function) with every non-exported declaration falling into one trailing unsorted bucket, which is what keeps that section's "a private type that exactly one helper uses may sit directly above that helper" exception followable rather than merely written; and `sort-classes` ranks fields → constructor → public methods → everything else. No other rule of the plugin is enabled and `@typescript-eslint/member-ordering` stays frozen out, since a second member-order authority is a second grammar for one claim. Two limits are recorded rather than discovered later: `sort-modules` publishes no variable selector and treats every module-level `const` as a **partition boundary** it moves nothing across, so §Module shape section (4) constant placement is convention and existing constants are preserved by construction; and the plugin's dependency ordering is populated for `class` and `enum` declarations only, so caller-above-callee among functions is never disturbed. Defaults left unset are load-bearing: `newlinesBetween: 'ignore'` and `partitionByComment` / `partitionByNewLine` `false` mean neither rule adds or removes a blank line. knip covers every workspace and `dependency-cruiser` is scoped to `apps/desktop`; both are CI gates on the tier-1 desktop job, run as two tasks with `--continue` so one red leg never hides the other's findings — the claims are disjoint (reachability from an entry point versus whether an import may exist) and neither subsumes the other. knip runs from the workspace root, because a package-scoped run sees neither the pnpm catalog nor the root tooling manifest and reports both as findings; it runs with `--treat-config-hints-as-errors`, so a pattern matching nothing fails the gate; it carries no `ignore` entry for an export: an export a document still claims and nothing imports yet carries a `@consumedBy <plain words>` tag naming no document or task, deleted in the change that imports it. dependency-cruiser cruises `src`, `build`, and `scripts` in one pass from the package root with `tsPreCompilationDeps`, so a type-only upward import is still a violation, and with an explicit `enhancedResolveOptions.extensions` list rather than `--ts-config`: it resolves a tsconfig's `extends` against the process directory, so a config whose `extends` climbs above its own directory loads only when the cruise runs from there, and this tree declares no path aliases, so nothing is lost. AVOID jscpd — its documented `minLines` / `minTokens` minimums (5 and 50) are the floor of a reported duplication and the duplicates this tree carries are one to four lines, while its tokenizer fires on the house style's near-identical block comments and deliberately parallel data tables; its publication record also carries a 557-day gap (4.0.5 on 2024-07-03 to 4.0.6 on 2026-01-11) with zero releases in calendar 2025, and v5 is a ground-up Rust rewrite shipping seven prebuilt native binaries through `optionalDependencies`. AVOID `eslint-plugin-boundaries` — under flat config it activates no resolver automatically and classifies an import no configured resolver resolves as `"external"`, so against this tree's `.js`-specifier imports it fails **open** and silently rather than loudly; making it correct would mean adding `eslint-import-resolver-typescript` for the `.js` → `.ts` alias map its own TypeScript-support guide requires, pulling the whole `eslint-plugin-import` resolver family in behind it. _The objection is that specific **resolver plumbing** and the fail-open it exists to repair, never a blanket rule that this stack admits no plugin: `eslint-plugin-perfectionist` resolves nothing — it reads one file's own AST — so it costs none of that and is admitted on the same row that refuses `eslint-plugin-boundaries`._ |
| Workflow definition file form | AVOID a codec library; the platform's own JSON parse and serialize are the whole of it | The file form is [Spec-015 §Definition file form — export and import (C-17)](./015-workflow-authoring-and-execution.md#definition-file-form--export-and-import-c-17)'s one canonical form, and no second dialect exists: a definition is one JSON document whose canonical bytes are the JCS canonicalization of its hashed body, carrying the document's own `schemaVersion` marker, whose V1 value is `2`, beside an optional layout section outside that body. The console's definition-detail export writes that document and its file-import authoring path reads it, both through the platform's own JSON parse and serialize, so no parsing library is admitted for this axis and no chunk is fetched for one. A document-model YAML codec was weighed and is **not** taken: it buys one thing, reading a marker scalar exactly as it was written, which a form with one JSON marker field does not need, and it would be a second definition of a dialect the corpus does not have. Import submits the parsed body through the daemon's ordinary create path, so an unknown schema version refuses with its registered code rather than being read optimistically, and the canonical bytes and the content hash stay the daemon's (Spec-015 §Authoring + versioning) rather than being recomputed in the renderer. |

### Console Test Tiers

The console's tests are tiered by what each proves, in the seven tiers below. Every tier but `assets`, which is a CI byte diff, is a Vitest project declared in `apps/desktop/vitest/tier-projects.ts`, the unit tier as the `renderer` project; the same file declares the `screenshot` capture aid and the `bench` ledger, which gate nothing, and `apps/desktop/vitest.config.ts` spreads them all beside its own `smoke` and `main-unit` projects. Each project has a glob no other project reaches, and no tier is optional for a console PR. No tier is a Playwright project: the tiers that need a real window drive Electron through Playwright's `_electron` launcher inside `tests/helpers/electron-harness.ts` and run under Vitest like every other tier.

| Tier | What it proves | Tooling |
| --- | --- | --- |
| unit | store transitions, projection arms, exhaustiveness, refusal grammar, and every pure derivation: the ship strip's next and alternate action from the daemon's facts and from the half-finished merge or rebase that takes one away, the live clock's text either side of sixty seconds, the child tree and its ancestry chain, the activity words per child state, the child state machine's transitions and their side effects, the per-identity fan-out of both stop acts from the daemon's own tree, the fork's title and worktree-name derivation and that name's validation, the markdown serializer behind copy, the console word table (each word, its caught spellings, the control it presses, the argument each control takes, and that one it cannot take is sent as typed), the address parser over every shape it accepts and every one it refuses, a mark's hit-test against the page snapshot's boxes, the renumbering that skips strokes, the page-eviction order, the cookie adapter between the two page hosts, and the two providers' browser-call shapes normalizing to one row, the settings route grammar over each of its arms, including a selection a page's own vocabulary does not know, the page list's pages and their order, the text-size step to root-size mapping, the transcript width's floor, ceiling and one-root step, each retention bound against the corpus figures, the chord-conflict refusal including the reserved chord, and the billing-mode total that spans two modes. Each region also renders every state this spec names from fixture data, the absent-and-grayed cases on a chat session among them, every settings page over its three read arms — in flight, refused and served — with the refused arm asserting the daemon's own words on screen, the token field asserted write-only in that it is never read back, never held in serializable state and cleared on submit, and the tool-server page's degraded arm asserting that the controls whose input never arrived are absent while every other control is offered as usual | Vitest with happy-dom |
| browser | geometry and pixel invariants: reading anchor, scroll chokepoint, reveal monotonicity, rect discipline. Headless, never a visible window and never taking focus, at a laptop width and again at a window narrow enough to fold the screen to one column: the scenario flows, plus the terminal pane, a pane-edge drag, keyboard resize, the double-click reset, width persistence, clamping at both limits, full width and its escape, compaction, the color-scheme cycle, the sessions search, opening a folder, the output-speed control, and the settings pass from the page list to a page, a control changed, a refusal and its rollback, with the derived-size rule proved by doubling the root. Every run requires zero page errors and zero console errors and asserts zero horizontal overflow in every pane on every screen | Vitest browser mode through `@vitest/browser-playwright` as its own project |
| end-to-end | flows declared as data over role and name locators, never test ids; tests named for the incident they reproduce. It also proves the embedded browser end to end: two clients attached to one page through the relay and driving it from both, with exact counter arithmetic and no detach events; a capture taken on a hidden view, asserting the scale is read from the returned image; the screencast's acknowledgment loop, asserting the stream does not stall after three frames; `connectOverCDP(transport)` over the relay listing exactly the open Preview pages with every console window absent, a page session's `Target.getTargets` and its attach to any other target refused, and no debug port open on the machine, which is the regression that catches the filter going missing or a `BrowserWindow` sneaking back in; and the process inventory — one renderer process per open page, one browser per machine, no per-session process beyond that, and the releases at the ten-minute mark and at the machine's memory ceiling. It proves the windows too, never in a visible window: two session views in one window and a view in a window of its own; a row press on a session shown elsewhere bringing that view forward and switching nothing; `⌘`-click, `Open beside`, `Open in new window` and a dragged title each landing the session in one place only; an address, `sidekicks open` and a notification click each landing where the session is already shown, else in the window used last; a pane's own window staying when the window it came from closes, and returning to its session's view or opening that session in the window used last; every window, its views and every pane window coming back after a restart; and closing every window stopping no session. The same undo run with two sessions in one folder puts back only its own session's files and lists the other's as `also changed by <session>`. The memory and processor budget of four windows showing eight sessions is measured against one window showing one | a Vitest project (`e2e`) driving a real Electron process through `@playwright/test`'s `_electron` launcher, against the `build:fixtures` artifact its Turbo task depends on; a separate check proves the release artifact's fuses are flipped |
| accessibility | zero violations on every route and dialog (WCAG 2.2 AA) | `axe-core` run in-page under the browser-mode project, bare rather than through `@axe-core/playwright` (§Console Libraries — browser-mode test code receives Vitest's own `page` locator object, and the Playwright `Page` that wrapper requires is reachable only inside a server-side custom command, where it is the orchestrator HTML around the test iframe); test-only MPL-2.0 dependency, [ADR-019 §License](../decisions/019-v1-deployment-model-and-oss-license.md#license) |
| endurance | flat heap over a thirty-minute multi-lane live stream | heap snapshots captured by an own CDP helper, analyzed with memlab; results in a dated ledger that records refutations |
| assets | generated tokens and schema artifacts byte-identical to their sources | CI byte diff |
| bundle | initial and lazy chunk sizes within `§Console Design (Meridian)`'s budgets | the `size-limit` bundle-size budget test (`tests/budget/`); dead exports are the `knip` structure gate's (§Console Libraries, structure enforcement) |

**Pixel comparison is not a gate.** No reference image is versioned, no CI job compares one, and no console PR is gated on pixels. What there is instead is a **local capture aid** — `pnpm --filter @ai-sidekicks/desktop run test:screenshot` writes every surface's capture, in both schemes, into the gitignored `apps/desktop/tests/screenshot/__screenshots__/` for a person to open and look at. It compares against nothing, belongs to no CI job and to no aggregate `test` chain, and fails only where a surface cannot be captured at all — an absent element, or a body still loading at the moment the capture would be taken. The capture is still taken through one chokepoint, `settled-capture.ts`, so what lands in the directory is a settled surface rather than a half-painted one.

**Color is asserted in all four renderings.** Every color-asserting check runs in each of the four theme-by-scheme cells; a layout check runs once, because layout is theme-invariant.

**Tripwires that must never regress.** The session-row status dots step rather than easing into a smooth pulse — a rule about those dots alone and never a global one; no status dot and no sessions control in the session header where the two columns are side by side; no goal element anywhere in the header or its menu; no worktree path inside a repository; no overflow menu on a child row; no text naming how a child was reached by its mechanism; no eyebrow over a user turn; no standing cost or quota figure outside the inspector's cost section and an agent row's own spend; and, in the `browser` pane, no browser-style tab strip in the pane chrome, no developer-tools control, no search, no dialog, overlay, backdrop or scrim, and no picture standing in for a real page on the machine that executes the session.

**A rule whose subject is not the screen is proved by the tier that owns it.** The saved site-data set and its per-site forget, the node-wide browser settings, and the process inventory are the main process's and the daemon's; every such rule is covered by the build's own tier for that layer rather than by a console check standing in for it. Four settings rules can only be shown by a running build and belong there in the same way: status arriving on the live governance stream, the tool-server page subscribing to that stream before it reads the inventory, a keyboard binding never reaching a wire, and the theme restored before the first paint.

**Two checks are re-run by hand after a version bump, never in continuous integration.** The dual-client probe — two clients on one page through the relay, the hidden-window screencast, the capture's scale — is re-run after an Electron or Playwright change. Provider parity is re-run after a provider change, on both providers with the same expected end state: the undo scenario (a file the agent added is gone, a file it changed is back, a file a shell command wrote is back) and the same with a background child's edit; the pause as a boundary interrupt, with the line reading that a command is still running until it ends; the interrupt with a command running, the row settling as stopped by the person on both; a foreground child surviving the lead's interrupt; the retry, the exhausted retry and the compaction; the limit row from a provider's own rate-limit frame; a child re-attached by its persisted identity after a daemon restart; and one browser call and one marks attachment on each provider producing the same transcript row in each provider's own image shape.

**Structural rules are not a test tier.** A structural rule — an import boundary, a chokepoint, a layout or naming convention, a one-home constraint — is enforced by the ESLint, `knip`, and `dependency-cruiser` configuration the §Console Libraries structure-enforcement row admits, or it is stated as a binding developer rule in `apps/desktop/AGENTS.md`, which every tool in the repository reads. **No tier reads source, configuration, or documentation text**; the tiers above prove product behavior against the running console.

**Renderer import direction.** The renderer's folders form a directed acyclic graph, low to high: `shared → lib / styles / assets → routing → components / hooks → store → services → registries → features → layout → app`. A folder may import any folder below it and none above it, and no feature imports another; the fix for an upward edge is to move the symbol down to the lowest folder that needs it, never to deep-import around the barrel. This order is owned by the `dependency-cruiser` gate, because an ordering over the folders is a graph property rather than a grep. **An upward edge is a CI failure, not a review note** — as is a cycle, an orphan module, an import from the renderer into `src/main/` or `src/preload/` or back, and an import from `src/shared/` into anything but the contracts package.

### Row-Cost Instrumentation

One flag, off by default and visible only to whoever sets it, logs what drawing the live rows costs in a running session — the middle case and the slow cases rather than every row — so a slowdown can be caught where it happens, in a real session, rather than only under the endurance tier's own load. It draws nothing, adds no control and no setting, and carries no figure onto a surface; it is a diagnostic for whoever turned it on.

### Renderer Bundle

React + Vite per ADR-015; TypeScript strict. Vite produces ES module output; the main process loads the bundle via a custom protocol (not `file://`, because the `GrantFileProtocolExtraPrivileges` fuse is disabled) with strict CSP (no `eval`, no inline scripts, no `unsafe-eval`). The same front end runs in the web client and the phone apps, in WebKit as well as Chromium, so Vite's `build.cssTarget` names the Chromium the desktop's Electron carries and the Safari and iOS versions those hosts carry, and the build writes `-webkit-user-select` beside `user-select`; no prefixed property is written by hand, and WebKit ignores the unprefixed form.

**Renderer scheme privileges.** The custom scheme the bundle is served from must be registered through `protocol.registerSchemesAsPrivileged` as `standard: true` and `secure: true` **before** `app.ready`. On a non-standard scheme Chromium exposes neither IndexedDB nor `localStorage`, so the console's persisted layouts and pins — UI state only; drafts are never persisted (`§Console Design (Meridian)` §Persistence on the renderer scheme) — would have no home; Plan-020 carries the registration as I-020-6.

**Renderer clipboard deprecation (Electron 40+):** Direct clipboard-API usage in renderer processes was deprecated in Electron 40. Clipboard access must be exposed via the preload bridge's `native.copyToClipboard` surface and implemented in the main process. The renderer must not call `navigator.clipboard.*` directly; CI lint must catch such imports.

## Pitfalls To Avoid

- **Giving the renderer any form of Node or process access.** `nodeIntegration: true` or `sandbox: false` in any window must be treated as a build-time error.
- **Leaking auth material across the bridge.** Audit the preload surface — any function returning a PASETO token (including the relay `connectionToken`), DPoP proof, or daemon session token to the renderer is a security regression. Relay negotiation runs in the background service; its `connectionToken` never reaches main and MUST NOT appear in any bridge response.
- **Skipping asar integrity verification in release builds.** `EnableEmbeddedAsarIntegrityValidation` must be enabled for every release; disabling it (even temporarily for debugging) in a signed release binary defeats the tamper-detection posture.
- **Coupling renderer lifecycle to daemon lifecycle.** Daemon disconnect must degrade the renderer to read-only mode, not crash it. Daemon crash with automatic restart must not lose renderer state.
- **Letting the renderer hold session-scoped state that should be re-derived from the daemon on reconnect.** State kept for fast UX (e.g., scroll position) is fine; state kept as truth (e.g., pending-approval list) creates divergence when the daemon reconnects.
- **Registering the `sidekicks://` protocol handler without conflict resolution.** On first run, if a prior installation already claims the handler, the new installation must either displace it explicitly or surface the conflict.
- **Treating the auto-update signature key as if it were the code-signing certificate.** They are separate keys with separate rotation schedules; conflating them risks a rotation mishap invalidating installed binaries.
- **Shipping renderer code that imports Node built-ins.** Vite's build must fail fast on accidental Node imports in the renderer bundle.
- **Polling the daemon on an interval from the renderer.** Reads happen on subscribe, on window focus, on reconnect, and on the terminal events the owning spec names; a renderer timer that re-reads on a cadence is the idle-CPU budget's first casualty and a second source of freshness the subscription already provides (`§Console Design (Meridian)`).
- **Projecting eligibility in the renderer.** A control the daemon may refuse is offered and its typed refusal is rendered; a renderer that pre-denies re-derives a rule it does not own, the `I-002-19` fail-closed-projection discipline every console surface generalizes.
- **Leaving fixture scenarios in a release bundle.** Fixture code and data — every scenario and each fixture beside the boundary it substitutes — sit behind the same compile-time `define` gate as the smoke probes in the next bullet, so a release build carries no fixture code, no catalog and no loader, and `--fixture` there is a startup error; a runtime `process.env` gate around any of them is rejected, not a style choice.
- **Embedding test-only code paths in production binaries.** Test-machinery (smoke probes, debug-only branches, internal instrumentation hooks) must be eliminated from release bundles via a compile-time-static gate — e.g., a Vite `define`-substituted identifier (`__SMOKE_BUILD__`-style) so Rollup's tree-shaker collapses `if (false && expr)` branches and physically strips the probe body from the shipped artifact. A runtime-only env-var check (`if (process.env.SOME_FLAG === '1')`) leaves the test code present in the bundle and weakens the §Trust Stance posture + earlier bullets in this section (every additional code path in a release binary is reachable surface). The runtime check is fine as defense-in-depth on the inner side of the compile-time gate, but never as the sole gate.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] The main process creates every window as a `BaseWindow` and every console document it shows as a `WebContentsView` with `contextIsolation: true`, `sandbox: true`, `nodeIntegration: false` and `webSecurity: true`; the renderer's own document carries the one preload script, which uses only `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld`; every further window's content — a window of session views or a pane's own window — is the renderer's `window.open` child, adopted in a `WebContentsView`, keeping the renderer's sandbox, isolation and content security policy and carrying no preload; every other `window.open` is denied; and no code path constructs a `BrowserWindow` — verified by contract test
- [ ] No auth material (daemon session token, PASETO tokens, DPoP key) appears on the `window.desktopBridge` surface — verified by a negative contract test against the desktop's preload API type
- [ ] Every Electron Fuse declared in §Security Hardening Baseline is set to the declared value in every released binary — verified by a build-time assertion
- [ ] Main starts the daemon detached (`child_process.spawn` with `detached`), never as a child of the app and never through `utilityProcess.fork`, reaches it through its socket, restarts it on a crash with the declared backoff, and surfaces the persistent-error state after five failed attempts — verified by integration test
- [ ] A quit flushes the service (`daemon.flush`) and leaves the service, every run and every terminal shell running: on macOS and Linux, after the app quits and after its process is sent SIGTERM or SIGKILL, the service still answers `daemon.hello` on its socket and a run started before the quit is still running; only Settings › Runtime's `Stop` and `Restart` end work — verified by integration test
- [ ] The update path is exercised end to end on every release platform in CI: a release whose version is not above the last one seen is refused; a downloaded file whose SHA-512 does not match the release's update file, or on Windows whose publisher does not match, is discarded; `Restart to finish` installs only a checked file; and after the restart the daemon handshake succeeds — verified by Plan-020 release-gate test
- [ ] Release artifacts are signed on every platform and pass its check: every macOS build carries the project's one self-signed identity and an update signed with it installs over the build before it, with notarization and Gatekeeper's pass once the Apple Developer ID certificate exists; on Windows 10 and 11, once SignPath Foundation's signing is in place, the NSIS installer and `sidekicks-windows-half.exe` carry its signature and pass SmartScreen's signed-file check; and on Linux the apt and dnf repositories' signatures verify on install and on update — verified by Plan-020 release smoke test
- [ ] Every Signature Feature view (the transcript, approvals, runs, the agents pane) composes the owning-plan contract without duplicating daemon logic — verified by renderer unit tests against mocked bridge methods
- [ ] The app's bundle size (post-asar, post-compression) is under the ADR-015 Success Criteria target of 150 MB — verified in CI by the `size-limit` check that measures each packaged installer with compression off
- [ ] The preload bridge surface has no `any`-typed escape hatch in its public type — verified by TypeScript strict-mode build
- [ ] Renderer attempts to access `require`, `process`, or `global` return `undefined` — verified by runtime assertion in a sandbox test
- [ ] Every window in main's registry of windows remains V8-reachable across the `app.whenReady().then(...)` callback unwind in a release-mode build, such that `window-all-closed` does not fire until the user closes the last window — verified by a `--js-flags=--expose-gc` lifecycle probe asserting (a) a `v8.queryObjects(BaseWindow)` count that holds stable across repeated GC pressure cycles and drops by at least one per window once every window is closed and the close has unwound — a per-window delta, so the fixed non-instance match a count-only sample cannot identify is never mistaken for the instance — AND (b) `window-all-closed` does not fire during the probe iteration loop. Per [ADR-023 §Antithesis — The Strongest Case Against](../decisions/023-electron-main-process-window-retention.md#antithesis--the-strongest-case-against), the load-bearing reachability mechanism is Electron's native-side `BaseWindow::self_ref_` (the `v8::Global<v8::Value>` field declared in `shell/browser/api/electron_api_base_window.h`, set in `BaseWindow::InitWith`, and reset only in `~BaseWindow` at native-object destruction — tag-exact lines in [ADR-023 §Primary sources (Electron v41.6.1)](../decisions/023-electron-main-process-window-retention.md#primary-sources-electron-v4161)); main's module-scope registry is defensive consistency with the canonical Electron community pattern, not the primary GC-anchor.
- [ ] Every message the person sent carries Copy, Fork, Edit and `Undo to here` on both providers and through a running turn, and is never without them; the pencil's resend and `Undo to here` go through the daemon's `session.restorePreview` and `session.restore`: the dry run's count shows before the undo asks, the turns after that point dim while a resend is in flight and stay in the flow marked superseded once it lands, a resend that fails puts the edited message back in the composer and leaves the conversation as it was, and an undo that applied only in part names what came back and what did not with its cause — every one of these rendered from the daemon's result and never re-derived in the renderer — verified by renderer unit tests against a mocked bridge
- [ ] The composer's command list offers only the entries enumerated under the session's own `(driverName, providerAccountId)` binding, and a change of that binding re-reads the enumeration rather than filtering the old one — a command enumerated on one provider is never listed for, or dispatched through, a session bound to the other.
- [ ] Each capability-gated composer affordance is **absent** rather than present-and-disabled where its driver flag is `false` — the compaction popover, the permission levels the level control lists and the rungs the effort control offers — with the one named exception §Rules every console surface obeys carries: the output-speed control, drawn on every session and grayed in place where the chosen model has no fast output, its reason both in its own hover title and in one small faint line beside the composer's chips naming that model. The compaction popover's completed state renders only on the daemon's compaction row rather than on the call returning.
- [ ] The MCP Servers page renders every value wire-verbatim from the `mcp.*` reads and the governance subscription, recomputes no effective idempotency class, renders no **withheld** configuration value — no environment-variable value, header value, URL query value, or token — while still rendering the daemon's redacted config read-back so the declaration can be inspected and edited, holds an OAuth launch URL as transient state that is persisted nowhere and dropped at settlement, and issues exactly one wire mutation per explicit act by the person.
- [ ] Every console surface renders in the Meridian design language of `§Console Design (Meridian)`: an agent's identity is carried by its hue, the person carries no identity mark, and attention is carried by amber and red alone; every wire figure renders in the mono face verbatim, and the two-hue rule and the contrast floors rule 3 of §Meridian, the design language sets are enforced at the token level — verified by the gallery-route accessibility sweep and by review against the gallery's own captures
- [ ] The console builds and its renderer tests run against fixtures, each beside the boundary it substitutes and answering only that boundary's operations; `--fixture <scenario> [--session <session-id>]` starts one of the fixture scenarios, and an unknown scenario, a missing value or a session the scenario does not hold stops startup with an error that names it; and a release bundle carries no fixture code, no catalog and no loader, so `--fixture` there is a startup error — verified by the release fixture gate (the release build's own source maps against the one fixture predicate, the imported-constant sweeps and the stylesheet dependency rule) and by a launch test that starts a release build with `--fixture transcript-states` and sees it exit with the startup error
- [ ] The budgets in `§Console Design (Meridian)` are measured on every console PR and a regression fails CI: renderer initial bundle ≤ 450 kB gzip, idle CPU ≤ 0.5 % of one core with a session open, renderer heap ≤ 120 MB with one session open at rest, and p95 frame time ≤ 16.7 ms with four lanes streaming — verified by the bundle gate and the endurance ledger
- [ ] `Mute notifications` in a session's actions list (`session.mute`) silences that session's `Finished` and `Failed` notifications on every channel and every device and never its `Waiting on you` or a workflow's Notify step, and `Unmute notifications` (`session.unmute`) undoes it; the switches `Waiting on you`, `Finished`, `Failed` and `Notify steps` under `Notify me outside the app` are kept per device in the machine's settings file, each stops only its own kind's banner on this machine, and the bell's list and the icon count are unchanged by either — verified by renderer unit tests against a mocked bridge
- [ ] Every window satisfies the first criterion in this list and is driven by the one renderer: a further window of session views and a pane's own window are the renderer's `window.open` children, answered by main's window-open handler with its own `BaseWindow` built from main's own options, carrying no preload, no bridge of their own and no auth material; main pairs the closes of the window and its child both ways; and no console window is ever in Preview's relay target list — verified by the multi-window end-to-end tier
- [ ] Any side pane — Review, Preview, the terminal, the file pane, the inspector and the agents pane — moves into a window of its own with `⌘⇧A` or the pane header's `Open in new window`, one window per pane per session, and the act on a pane already in its own window brings that window forward; `Return to session`, `⌘⇧A` pressed in that window and closing it each return the pane to its session's view, or open the session in the window used last where it is on screen nowhere; the pane's window belongs to its session and stays when the window it came from closes; a Preview page moves whole with its pane, with no reload, the agent's session and the screencast carrying on; and a pane in its own window at quit comes back in its own window — verified by the multi-window end-to-end tier
- [ ] A window holds one or more session views side by side; each session shows in one view on this device and each destination in one window, and asking for either again, by any route, brings its place forward; a session's address, `sidekicks open`, a notification click and a bell line bring forward the view that shows the session, or else switch the focused view of the window used last to it, or open a window on it when none is open; a window's title is its focused view's `<session title> — <project>`, and a pane's own window's `<Pane> — <session title>`; every window, its views with their order, widths and focus, and every pane's own window come back after a restart and after a renderer crash; closing a view or every window stops no session; and the memory and processor cost of four windows showing eight sessions is measured against one window showing one — verified by the multi-window end-to-end tier
- [ ] No debug port is open on either host: neither `--remote-debugging-port` nor `--remote-debugging-pipe` is set; the agent's browser tools reach desktop pages only through the daemon's relay over Electron's in-process debugger, whose target list is exactly the open Preview pages with every console window absent; and on a page's session every `Target.*` and `Browser.*` command outside the page-session filter is refused — verified by the Electron tier's end-to-end test

## Open Questions

- **Windows 10 support horizon:** Windows 10 reached end of support on 2025-10-14. V1 supports Windows 10 and 11, x64 and arm64. Whether a later release supports Windows 11 only is a scope decision for the project's owner, not a technical blocker.

### Console Design Sources

The rows below index the sources per axis behind `§Console Design (Meridian)`, §Console Libraries, §Console Test Tiers, and §Electron Version And Support Window. Type is one of: registry, upstream source, upstream docs, issue, changelog, measurement (local, in the repository's own Electron binary), license text, spec.

**Measurement record for the `Measured (ours)` rows.** Every `Measured (ours)` row in D.1–D.10 was measured on 2026-09-01, unless the row names its own date, on the reference development machine (Apple silicon, macOS 26, Node 24.18.0) by the method the row states or this record does: bundle rows use esbuild (`--bundle --minify --format=esm --platform=browser --target=es2022`, or the `chrome146` target where the row says so; `react` / `react-dom` / `react/jsx-runtime` externalized; gzip level 9; "total" summing the entry plus every async chunk emitted); heap rows use `node --expose-gc` retained-heap deltas after a forced collection; timing rows are warm-process medians over the corpus the row names (the 64 KB markdown message, the 10k-line TypeScript file and diff, the 20 000-entity map with 300 subscribers, the 100 000-string search corpus, the 120-column 10 000-line terminal scrollback); renderer probes ran inside the Electron binary the row names. No harness script is retained, so these rows are reproducible by method rather than by artifact; every verdict they drive also rests on a non-measured leg — a license, a maintenance signal, an API fact, a security posture — recorded beside them. The durable artifact is Plan-020 T-020p-1C-8's committed benchmark tier, which re-measures the **adopted** set under version control — bundle bytes on every CI run through `size-limit`, and the fan-out, fuzzy-search, markdown tail-slice, and xterm throughput micro-benchmarks as the endurance ledger's timing rows — so every figure a shipped verdict rests on is re-derived from that task forward, while figures for libraries this spec rejects stay as recorded here and are re-measured only when such a library is reconsidered.

**D.1 Timeline virtualization**

| Source | Type | Key Finding | URL |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Chromium `LayoutUnit` (Blink; the pinned Electron 44.5.1 ships Chromium 152.0.7977.130) | Source file | `kIntMax = kRawValueMax / kFixedPointDenominator` = 2147483647 / 64 = 33,554,431 px maximum element height, so 100k rows at 300 px (30 M px) sit at 89% of the ceiling and the window cap is mandatory. | https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/geometry/layout_unit.h |
| CSS Scroll Anchoring Module | Spec | Anchoring is suppressed by any change to `top/left/right/bottom`, margin, padding, width/height, `position`, or `transform` on the anchor-to-scroller path, which every virtualizer's row positioning triggers, so sub-row anchoring must be own-built. | https://drafts.csswg.org/css-scroll-anchoring/ |
| `@tanstack/react-virtual` npm manifest | Registry | 3.14.10 published 2026-08-18 (core 3.17.8), MIT, peer React ^16.8 through ^19, ESM+CJS with types, 23,240,523 weekly downloads. | https://registry.npmjs.org/@tanstack%2Freact-virtual/latest |
| TanStack Virtualizer API docs | Docs | `getScrollElement`, `scrollToFn`, `observeElementRect`, and `observeElementOffset` are all overridable options and `shouldAdjustScrollPositionOnItemSizeChange` gates the only other scroll write, so our chokepoint can be the sole `scrollTop` writer. | https://tanstack.com/virtual/latest/docs/api/virtualizer |
| `@tanstack/virtual-core@3.16.0` release | Changelog | `anchorTo: 'end'` landed 2026-05-25 and "keeps an end-pinned viewport pinned when the last item grows during streaming output" while keeping the visible item stable on prepend. | https://github.com/TanStack/virtual/releases/tag/%40tanstack%2Fvirtual-core%403.16.0 |
| `@tanstack/virtual-core@3.17.6` release | Changelog | Re-measurements now compensate only items entirely above the fold (`itemStart + itemSize <= scrollOffset`), fixing #1218 where a fold-spanning streaming message dragged `scrollTop` token by token, so anything older is broken for streaming. | https://github.com/TanStack/virtual/releases/tag/%40tanstack%2Fvirtual-core%403.17.6 |
| TanStack React adapter docs | Docs | React 19 can log "flushSync was called from inside a lifecycle method" unless `useFlushSync: false`, and `directDomUpdates` (3.14.0) skips React on scroll ticks, which PR #1141 measured at 407 to 215 renders on a 200x100 px scripted scroll. | https://tanstack.com/virtual/latest/docs/framework/react/react-virtual |
| TanStack issue #1221 | Issue | Open: no public API to cancel or retarget the rAF reconcile loop armed by `scrollTo*`/`scrollBy`, so our controller must not depend on smooth `scrollToIndex`. | https://github.com/TanStack/virtual/issues/1221 |
| `virtual-core/src/index.ts` and `react-virtual/src/index.tsx` | Source file | One lazily created `ResizeObserver` per virtualizer with `elementsCache` entries deleted when a node is no longer connected; in the React adapter `useFlushSync` defaults to `true`. | https://github.com/TanStack/virtual/blob/main/packages/virtual-core/src/index.ts |
| TanStack first-party benchmark README | Benchmark | Medians of 5 Chromium runs: mount-fixed-100k tanstack 6.1 ms vs virtua 3.1 ms, memory 14.2 MB vs 10.5 MB, virtuoso 188 ms dynamic settle and 154 ms jump-to-end, all four at 60 fps on a scroll the README calls "too gentle to expose perf differences". | https://github.com/TanStack/virtual/blob/main/benchmarks/README.md |
| React Aria comparison section of the same README | Benchmark | Full `react-aria-components` Virtualizer stack mounts in 175.2 ms at 100k (vs 5.8 ms headless TanStack), uses 99.7 MB, and returns a `-1` landing error because it exposes no `scrollToIndex`. | https://github.com/TanStack/virtual/blob/main/benchmarks/README.md |
| esbuild 0.27.3 bundle measurement (min, ESM, `chrome146`, React externalized, gzip -9) | Measured (ours) | `@tanstack/react-virtual` 7.6 kB gz, `virtua` `VList` 4.2 kB, `react-virtuoso` `Virtuoso` 20.0 kB, `react-window` `List`+`useDynamicRowHeight` 3.7 kB, `react-virtualized` `List`+`CellMeasurer`+`AutoSizer` 28.4 kB, `use-stick-to-bottom` 2.7 kB. | https://esbuild.github.io/api/#bundle |
| `virtua` `src/core/driver.ts` | Source file | The driver itself calls `viewport.scrollTo({behavior:'instant'})` and `scrollBy` for jump compensation with no opt-out, so it would fight an external scroll chokepoint. | https://github.com/inokawa/virtua/blob/main/src/core/driver.ts |
| virtua issue #901 | Issue | Open: `flushSync` inside the `ResizeObserver` path triggers a Blink `FATAL ... StateAllowsTreeMutations()` crash when a contenteditable caret paints in the same frame. | https://github.com/inokawa/virtua/issues/901 |
| virtua issue #301 | Issue | Open since 2023-12-28: scroll position does not stay at the bottom when the viewport height changes, with the maintainer's 2024-01 reply "It's planned but not right away". | https://github.com/inokawa/virtua/issues/301 |
| `react-virtuoso` `hooks/useChangedChildSizes.ts` | Source file | A single `ResizeObserver` watches the items container and every callback walks `el.children` reading `offsetHeight` of each mounted row, so N streaming lanes force O(mounted rows) layout reads per resize. | https://github.com/petyosi/react-virtuoso/blob/main/packages/react-virtuoso/src/hooks/useChangedChildSizes.ts |
| React Virtuoso troubleshooting docs | Docs | "If you have zero-height items, you need to filter those out before passing them to the component ... There's no way to fix this", and `contentRect` measurement ignores margins. | https://virtuoso.dev/react-virtuoso/troubleshooting |
| react-virtuoso issue #1354 | Issue | Open since 2026-02-09: using `alignToBottom` with `firstItemIndex` prepends makes the list flicker to the top of the area. | https://github.com/petyosi/react-virtuoso/issues/1354 |
| `@virtuoso.dev/message-list` npm manifest | Registry | 1.17.2 (2026-08-28) declares `"license": "Commercial"` and requires a `VirtuosoMessageListLicense` key. | https://registry.npmjs.org/@virtuoso.dev%2Fmessage-list/latest |
| `react-window` `lib/components/list/List.tsx` | Source file | The `List` renders its own `overflowY: "auto"` scroll element and hard-codes `role="list"` / `role="listitem"` with `aria-posinset`/`aria-setsize`, so it cannot attach to an external scroller. | https://github.com/bvaughn/react-window/blob/main/lib/components/list/List.tsx |
| react-window issue #883 | Issue | Open: maintainer states "I don't think there's a good way for me to improve this use case" for `scrollToRow` landing on dynamic-height rows because the target offset is only an estimate. | https://github.com/bvaughn/react-window/issues/883 |
| `react-virtualized` README and npm manifest | Docs | README steers users to `react-window` "as a possible lighter-weight alternative"; latest 9.22.6 was published 2025-01-20 with zero releases in the last 12 months and no bundled types. | https://github.com/bvaughn/react-virtualized/blob/master/README.md |
| MDN `content-visibility` | Docs | With `content-visibility: auto` skipped content remains in the DOM and accessibility tree and is findable and focusable, so all 100k rows stay as live DOM nodes. | https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/content-visibility |
| CSSWG issue #9833 | Issue | Open: scroll-into-view targets drift as `content-visibility: auto` children swap `contain-intrinsic-size` for real size mid-scroll, so the CSS-only approach would undermine an anchor controller. | https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/9833 |
| `use-stick-to-bottom` `src/useStickToBottom.ts` | Source file | The 665-line hook sets `scrollRef.current.scrollTop` itself and animates with a spring (`damping 0.7`, `stiffness 0.05`, `mass 1.25`) over non-virtualized content, so it competes with both the chokepoint and any virtualizer. | https://github.com/samdenty/use-stick-to-bottom/blob/main/src/useStickToBottom.ts |

| Layout determinism + quality benchmark (dagre 3.1.1, d3-dag 1.2.2, elkjs 0.12.0, own) | Benchmark | On phase-sequence DAGs all four produced identical coordinate hashes across two processes and zero crossings/overlaps; at 200 nodes best times were own 0.3 ms, d3-dag 21.5 ms, dagre 31.1 ms, elkjs 48–51 ms, with own matching dagre's 44,080 × 472 extent and ELK 2,415 tall. The pick this evidence settled on is `@dagrejs/dagre` (§Console Libraries): the layout runs in the renderer and in the daemon alike, so one maintained library on both sides is what makes the two processes agree on where a node sits, and every candidate clears the determinism and quality bar the canvas needs ([Spec-015 §Implementation Notes](./015-workflow-authoring-and-execution.md#implementation-notes)). | https://eclipse.dev/elk/reference/algorithms/org-eclipse-elk-layered.html |
| Spec-015 §Visual Workflow Builder (SA-31, SA-34) and ADR-024 | Spec | The graph is the phase set plus one entry node with the sequence edge as the sole edge kind, layout is excluded from the hashed body and persists client-local, and a layout-less definition renders through "a deterministic topological auto-layout derived from the phase sequence". | docs/specs/015-workflow-authoring-and-execution.md |

### Console Surface Sources

The sources behind `§Console Design (Meridian)`'s surface set, its pane and window model, and the daemon-side library rows in §Console Libraries.

- Electron `dialog` API reference — `showOpenDialog`'s `openFile`, `openDirectory` and `multiSelections` properties, and the platform note that one picker cannot offer both files and folders on Windows or Linux; read 2026-09-14. Behind the composer's attach control and its files-only, several-at-once, never-a-folder rule.
- Two Chrome-debug clients on one page, measured 2026-10-01 on Electron 44.5.1, Chromium 152, `playwright-core` 1.63.0, macOS arm64: both clients attach in either order without error, the counter arithmetic is exact with no lost or doubled input, each observes the other's mutations, and the in-process debugger emits no detach event, so neither client needs exclusive ownership of the page. A never-shown window screencasts at 51 to 54 frames a second with zero acknowledgment errors while an external client drives the same page, with background throttling off and painting when initially hidden on; with no external client attached it gives no frames until focus emulation is on (85 to 94 frames a second once it is). The acknowledgment method must be called by its exact name, or the queue stalls after exactly three frames, which is the frames-in-flight default. A `BrowserWindow`'s never-navigated page target blocks an external attach forever (thirty-second timeout), where a `BaseWindow` completes the same attach in 37 to 48 milliseconds with four processes rather than five. A capture on a never-shown window returns a genuinely doubled image on a display whose scale factor is two while the platform reports a single scale factor, so the scale is read from the returned image. One hidden page view costs about 243 MB of working set and three extra processes over a bare main process, and driving both clients through two screencasts adds about 112 MB; on macOS at this Electron the process metrics carry only the working-set figures, so the working set is the figure read.
- Shared emulation state, same measurement: a connected external automation client unconditionally pins focus emulation and a light color scheme for as long as it is attached, so a screencast running underneath visibly changes theme and focus behavior. Emulation state, device metrics and network interception are therefore shared mutable state the daemon owns and re-asserts after any client detaches; evaluation, input and the screencast need no such ownership.
- Development-server discovery has no library: every candidate a registry search surfaces checks whether a port is _available_ rather than enumerating listening sockets with their owning process identities, so the daemon does it itself — three platform branches over the machine's listening sockets plus a short-timeout loopback probe, one probe loop per daemon, ticking only while a project session is live.
- Registry facts read 2026-09-21 for the libraries §Console Libraries admits on the daemon side and for the renderer's mark strokes, `playwright-core` read again 2026-10-01: `playwright-core` 1.63.0 (Apache-2.0; 13.5 MB unpacked; `@playwright/mcp` 0.0.80, 86 kB, read and not admitted), `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.30.0 (MIT, 4.3 MB), `perfect-freehand` 1.2.3 (MIT, 112 kB, no runtime dependencies), `@dagrejs/dagre` 3.1.1 (MIT, 1.4 MB), `croner` 10.0.1 (MIT, 155 kB, no dependencies, Node ≥ 18), `picomatch` 4.0.7 (MIT, 94 kB), and `mermaid` 11.17.2 (MIT, 84.0 MB unpacked and loaded lazily; 12.0.0 published 2026-09-10 is a separate, evidenced bump).
